Paul says that he has glory before God through Jesus Christ; for by believing and serving Christ Jesus with a pure conscience, he earned merit before God the Father, to such an extent that he says nothing is lacking that Christ did not accomplish through him for the call of the Gentiles, giving signs and wonders through his hands, so that power would extol his preaching. Therefore he shows that he has glory in Christ before God; for by serving Christ he has glory before God, to such an extent that he cannot lack any divine power that has not been granted to him by God. Rather, he professes that, because he was found to be a worthy steward, he obtained everything that would advance the conversion of the Gentiles through the power of signs. This serves to prove that he was no less capable than the other apostles who had been with the Lord, and that God worked no lesser deeds of power among the Gentiles, so that by this very fact they might increase, seeing that they had attained the grace which the Jews had attained, who claim for themselves the privilege of the Fathers.

Romans 15:20 So that from Jerusalem and round about as far as Illyricum I have fully preached the Gospel of God and of His Son, Christ. Therefore I have preached the Gospel, not where Christ had already been named, lest I should build upon another’s foundation. 

Paul does not say without reason that he strives to preach there where Christ has not been named; for knowing that the false apostles were handing on Christ differently than they ought, for this was why they went about, so that under the name of Christ they might subject the people to other doctrines, which later was exceedingly difficult to correct, he therefore wished to forestall them, so that he might deliver to his hearers the words of an uncorrupted preaching, and so that the entire line of the arranged surface might have the foundation’s strength intact. And since he was appointed teacher of the Gentiles, he ought diligently to ensure this: that he teach there where Christ had not been proclaimed, so as both to establish his authority and to obtain the full fruit of his labors from those whom he had planted. Therefore the Church occupied all places; for afterward heretics, plotting with evil subtlety, endeavored under the name of Christ to corrupt the meaning of the Law and of the faith. This he warned by the testimony of the Law, saying:

Romans 15:21 But as it is written: “Those who have not been told about him will see, and those who have not heard will understand”;

This is found in Isaiah (Isa. 62:15). Therefore, so that this understanding might be true and firm concerning the true Son of God, Paul says that he always hastened to fill the nations with evangelical truth:

Romans 15:22-24 Therefore I was greatly hindered from coming to you. But now, having no further place in these regions, and having had for many previous years a desire to come to you, when I begin my journey to Spain, I shall see you, and be escorted there by you, if I may first enjoy your company for a while.

This is what Paul mentions at the beginning of the letter, saying: “Because I have often purposed to come to you, and have been hindered until now” (Rom. 1:13). Now he explains the reason why he was hindered: although wishing to come, he was engaged in another task that was pressing, namely, to refute the wicked arguments of the false apostles. Finally, after preaching throughout all the surrounding regions, he says that he is now free to come to Rome, which he had formerly desired. Therefore, because the Romans had been led into the Law, he corrects them meanwhile through this letter. But those who had not yet heard the preaching would be better instructed by his presence, so that, being founded in the true faith, they would be less likely to accept anything else. Nevertheless, he promised that he would come at the time when he was going to Spain, because Christ had not been preached there, so that he might occupy their minds; and because it was difficult for the false apostles to travel to them, there was no harm if he went later.

Romans 15:25-26 But now I am setting out for Jerusalem to minister to the saints; for Macedonia and Achaia have decided to make some contribution for the poor among the saints who are in Jerusalem. 

Yet Paul says that he will first go to minister to the saints in Jerusalem, by which he wants the Romans to know that such works must be supported: for those who live by mercy and have been justified before God ought to serve this cause.

Romans 15:27 For they are their debtors; since if the Gentiles have become partakers of their spiritual things, they ought also to minister to them in material things. 

Indeed, Paul calls them debtors to the believing Jews, so that just as they have become partakers of their spiritual things, so they too may share in their bodily needs; that, rejoicing, the believing Jews may praise together the providence of God for their salvation through their ministry. For these, devoting themselves entirely to divine services and caring for nothing worldly, offered believers an example of good conduct. Finally, the Apostle wishes us to be so caring toward mercy that he calls us debtors to give alms and to practice good works with eagerness of heart: because whoever hopes for mercy from God ought to be merciful, thereby proving that he rightly hopes for it; for if a human being shows mercy, how much more God! For this is the recompense or reward: that those who receive mercy should show mercy. Therefore the Lord says: “Blessed are the merciful” (Matt. 5:7); “for God will have mercy on them.”

Romans 15:28-29 When I have completed this and delivered this fruit to them, I shall depart for Spain by way of you. For I know that, when I come to you, I shall come in the abundance of Christ’s blessing. 

Confident, therefore, in God’s arrangement and grace, Paul promised that he would come in the abundance of the blessing which he preaches of Christ. This blessing, however, is the power of signs, by which they have been confirmed.

Romans 15:30-31 I ask you therefore, brethren, by our Lord Jesus Christ and by the love of the Holy Spirit, to share my concern in prayers to the Lord for me, that I may be delivered from the unbelievers who are in Judea. 

Then Paul prays that he may be helped by their prayers, so that he might escape the hands of the unbelieving Jews, not because he himself deserves it less, but he follows the order that the Church should pray for its leader. For many insignificant people, when they gather unanimously, become great; and it is impossible for the prayers of many not to obtain their request. If they too are eager to see the Apostle, let them pray more fervently, so that, once delivered, they may receive him with the joy of love.

Romans 15:32 And that the ministry of my gifts may be accepted by the saints in Jerusalem; that I may come to you with joy through the will of Jesus Christ, and be refreshed with you. 

Paul also says that this should be prayed for: that the ministry of his gifts may be accepted by the saints in Jerusalem, and that he may show himself to do all things according to the will of God; that, because his heart is devoted to bearing the gifts, he also wishes their attitude toward him to correspond to God’s judgment; that, having understood his love toward them, they may with one accord give thanks to God with him. For it is a great gain for him when, made joyful by his ministry, they praise God.

Romans 15:33 May the God of peace be with you all. Amen.

The God of peace is Christ, who says: “I give you my peace, I leave you peace” (Jn. 14:17), whom Paul desires to be with these, knowing that the Lord said: “Behold, I am with you all days until the end of the age” (Matt. 28:20). Therefore he desires them to be such that the Lord Christ Jesus may be with them, who, having cut off all conflict arising from human error, gave and showed what is true, so that they may remain peaceful in that very truth.

Chapter 16

Romans 16:1-2 I commend to you Phoebe our sister, who is a minister of the Church at Cenchreae; that you receive her in the Lord worthily of the saints, and assist her in whatever matters she may need you; for she herself also has assisted many, and me.

Paul commends Phoebe, who is coming, as a common sister, that is, according to the Law. To commend her suitably, he says that she is a minister of the Church at Cenchreae; and because she was a help to many, he says that she should rightly be helped on account of her journey. But so that he might persuade them to receive her without delay and assist her in her need, if she arrived, he also testifies that she had assisted him; so that, the more distinguished her person is than that of others, the more he might show that the service owed to her should be rendered through love.

Romans 16:3-5a Greet Prisca and Aquila, my helpers in Christ Jesus, who laid down their necks for my life; to whom not only I give thanks, but also all the Churches of the Gentiles, and their household Church. 

These were Jews who, having become believers, became fellow laborers with the Apostle; they believed so rightly that they themselves also encouraged the others to the true faith. Finally, although Apollos was well versed in the Scriptures, he was nevertheless more carefully instructed by them in the way of the Lord: therefore he calls them his fellow laborers, but in Christ Jesus; for they were his fellow workers in the Gospel of God. Aquila is Prisca’s husband, and it is clear that they did not come to Rome without purpose; for they were more devoted to the worship of God: indeed, for the strengthening of the Romans, all those whom he greets are understood to have been from among them. Therefore Paul says that not only he, but also all the Churches of the Gentiles, give them thanks, also referring to the Romans, so that they may obey those whom they hear laboring for the advancement of the Gentiles, to encourage them to faith in Christ. He praises them so highly that they did not refuse to suffer dangers for him; for they did not disdain to endure the hostility of both the Jews and false brethren, provided that they might assist the Apostle with their service and charity. The false brethren were those who believed in Christ, yet said that the Law had to be observed, thinking that Christ was not sufficient for the full effect of salvation; this the Apostle was destroying, and therefore he suffered persecutions from them. He also greets their household members and servants, whom he calls a Church, because they were disciples of holy men in the faith.

Romans 16:5b Greet Epaenetus, beloved by me, who is the first fruits of Asia in Christ. 

Paul does not conceal Epaenetus’s present dignity either, to show that men of rank also believe, and to invite the leading Romans to the faith, or certainly so that the humble might grow.

Romans 16:6 Greet Mary, who labored much among you. 

Paul reminds these by the name of Mary, whom we understand to have labored especially diligently for their encouragement, so that they may give her thanks.

Romans 16:7 Greet Andronicus and Julia, my kinsmen and fellow captives, who are distinguished among the apostles, who were in Christ before me. 

These kinsmen, both according to the flesh and according to the spirit, as the angel also said to Mary: “Behold, Elizabeth your kinswoman,” etc. (Luke 1:36), whom Paul also declares to have served the earlier apostles with their testimony, and to have suffered captivity together with him for the sake of the faith; and therefore they are to be honored more fully.

Romans 16:8 Greet my beloved in Christ with an expanded greeting. 

Paul greets him as a friend, but a friend in the Lord; yet not one who shared in his labor or captivity.

Romans 16:9a Greet Urban, my helper in Christ. 

Paul calls him not only his own helper, but also a companion of the others in the exhortation to faith.

Romans 16:9b Also Stachys, my beloved. 

Although Paul calls this Stachys beloved, he nevertheless places him with Urban as a participant in the work of the Gospel.

Romans 16:10 Greet Appelles, approved in Christ. 

Paul greets this Appelles not as his friend or partner in work, but because he was tested by trials and found faithful in Christ.

Romans 16:11a Greet those who are of the household of Aristobulus. 

This Aristobulus is understood to have been a gatherer of the brethren in Christ; and Paul proves this by labeling those whom he gathered as worthy of his greeting.

Romans 16:11b Greet Herodion, our kinsman. 

By calling him only a kinsman, Paul shows him to be devoted to the love of rebirth; yet he did not indicate his watchfulness.

Romans 16:11c Greet those who are of the household of Narcissus, who are in the Lord. 

This Narcissus is said to have been a presbyter at that time, as is read in other manuscripts. And because Paul was not present, you see why he greets as holy, in the Lord, those who were of his household. This presbyter Narcissus, however, performed the office of a traveler, strengthening believers with encouragements; and since the Apostle did not know the merits of those who had been with him, he said: Greet those who are of the household of Narcissus in the Lord; that is, greet in my name those whom you know to be worthy among them of my greeting, who have placed their hope in the Lord.

Romans 16:12a Greet Tryphaena and Tryphosa, who labor in the Lord. 

Paul declares these two worthy of equal honor in Christ.

Romans 16:12b Greet the beloved Persis, who labored greatly in the Lord. 

She appears to be placed before those mentioned above because she labored greatly in the Lord. This labor consists in encouragement, in ministry to the saints, and in distress and poverty for Christ’s sake; for they were abandoning their homes when driven out, and were a reproach to the unbelievers.

Romans 16:13 Greet Rufus, chosen in the Lord, and his mother and mine. 

For this reason he placed that Rufus before his mother because of the election for the administration of God’s grace, in which a woman has no place; for he was chosen, that is, promoted by the Lord to carry out His affairs. Nevertheless, he regarded his mother as so holy that the Apostle also called her his own mother.

Romans 16:14 Greet Asyncletus, Phlegon, Herman, Patrobas, Hermas, and the brothers who are with them. 

Paul greets them together because he knew them to be united in Christ, that is, joined in Christian fellowship. He likewise greets the brothers who were with them, whose names he omitted.

Romans 16:15 Greet Philologus, and Julia, and Nereus, and his sister Olympias, and all the saints who are with them. 

All these are understood to have been united in mind, whom he greets together for this reason; their merit is recognized from those who were with them. For Paul calls them saints, so that he may rightly be seen to greet them.

Romans 16:16a Greet one another with a holy kiss. 

Paul commands all to whom he writes, and whom he names, to greet one another with a holy kiss, that is, in the peace of Christ, not in the desire of the flesh, but in the Holy Spirit; so that the kisses may be religious, not carnal.

Romans 16:16b All the Churches of Christ greet you. 

By this it is understood that there is also said to be a Church which is not of Christ. Therefore David calls the conspiracy of the wicked the Church of the wicked (Ps. 25:5). Therefore Paul says that the Churches of all those places greet them, so that they may grow in faith. This, then, he referred to the preceding meaning, to show that Christ is he in whom salvation is, and that this is he whose people are faithful, and by whose will every creature lives. For Christ is the author of life, as the apostle Peter says (Acts 3:15), not the Law, in which some of them thought they ought to hope.

Romans 16:17-19a Now I urge you, brothers, to watch out for those who cause dissensions and scandals contrary to the teaching you have learned; avoid them. For such people do not serve Christ our Lord Jesus, but their own appetites; and through enticing and flattering speech they deceive the hearts of the innocent. For your obedience has been reported everywhere. 

Here Paul suddenly speaks of the persons of the false apostles, whom throughout the epistle he warned them to beware of, as he does now; but without revealing them, he restrained their tradition. For they compelled believers to Judaize, so that they might nullify God’s benefit toward them, as I mentioned above; for, composing genealogical arguments, they adapted teachings to praise their tradition, by which they might deceive the hearts of the simple.  “For your obedience,” he says, “has been reported everywhere.” This is what he says at the beginning of the epistle (Rom. 1:8): “because your faith is proclaimed throughout the whole world.” This means that he is confident of their obedience. For it was unbelievable that, after recognizing the truth, they would submit themselves to something foolish as though they were wise.

Romans 16:19b Therefore I rejoice in you. 

Here too the meaning is drawn from the preceding part of the epistle; for Paul rejoices because the Romans, who seem to possess dominion, have submitted themselves to the Christian faith.

Romans 16:19c I want you to advance, so that you may be learned in what is good, but inexperienced in what is evil. 

The learned in good here is one who does good works; the inexperienced in evil are those who are ignorant of evil deeds, that is, who do not know the practice of wickedness.

Romans 16:20a But the God of peace will crush Satan under your feet swiftly;

That is, shortly. Paul says this concerning his coming, because his coming would oppress the Devil on account of the fact that these people were going to receive spiritual grace; Satan envies this, because he wants people to remain in error; the rest differs, although they are of one profession.

Romans 16:20b The grace of our Lord Jesus Christ be with you. 

The grace which he promised them at his coming, he now desires to be with them; for if they deserve to receive it, it is already with them in hope.

Romans 16:21 Timothy, my fellow laborer, greets you, as do Lucius, Jason, and Sosipater, my relatives. 

Timothy is his fellow laborer, as it were a co-bishop, who governed the Church with great concern and was exposed to the envy of the Jews to such an extent that, at the beginning, he was circumcised because of the Jews’ scandal; since his mother was Jewish, he could not be a teacher while uncircumcised. Paul calls them relatives partly because of lineage and partly because of faith.

Romans 16:22 I, Tertius, greet you, who wrote this letter in the Lord. 

Tertius by name, not by number: he is the scribe of the letter, whom Paul allowed to greet the Roman people in his own name, to whom he writes, so that they may greet the others whom he names. For at that time there were still few leaders in the churches.

Romans 16:23 Caius, my host, greets you, and so do all the Churches. 

This is Caius, I believe, to whom the apostle John writes (3 John 1), rejoicing in his love, which he showed to the brethren by providing them with the expenses necessary. And whereas above he says: “All the Churches of Christ greet you”; now he repeats, saying: “All the Churches greet you,” which I do not think was repeated without purpose; for so great and methodical a man placed nothing unnecessary. But because Paul says, “All the Churches of Christ greet you,” that is, all the saints, as he says elsewhere: “Those who are Christ’s have crucified their flesh” (Gal. 5:24); now he added that these also greet you, who are followers; so that he might signify the blessed people of the Churches, because in every Church there are two peoples. He may also have meant the Churches of two provinces, saying first “all the Churches” of one place, then of another, or certainly those of the Jews and the Gentiles.

Romans 16:24 Erastus, steward of the city, and Quartus, a brother, greet you. 

The city steward is like a keeper who, guided by justice, governed the city, especially in regulating prices. Paul names all of these individually in the greeting to the Romans so that they might know the kind and number of those who rejoiced together with them in their good undertaking.

Romans 16:25-27 But to Him who is able to strengthen you according to my Gospel and the preaching of Jesus Christ, according to the revelation of the mystery that was kept in silence through eternal times, but is now revealed through the prophetic Scriptures, according to the command of the eternal God, for obedience to the faith among all nations, made known to the only wise God through Jesus Christ, to whom be glory forever and ever. Amen.

Let him give glory to God the Father, from whom all things are, so that He may consent to fulfill what was begun among the Romans, since He is powerful, by confirming their hearts in faith for the advancement of the Gospel and the revelation of the mystery hidden from the ages but revealed through Christ, or in Christ (Eph. 3:9). For the mystery that was always hidden in God was revealed in the time of Christ; because God is not solitary, since with Him from eternity is the Paraclete, in whose truth He decreed that every creature should be saved through knowledge. The truth of this mystery had indeed been indicated by the prophets through certain figures, known only to the wise God, whose grace He willed the nations to share, though it had been hidden from humankind. He alone, therefore, is wise, because all wisdom is from Him, as Solomon says: “All wisdom is from the Lord God, and it has always been with Him” (Eccles. 1:1). This Wisdom is Christ, because it is from Him and was always with Him, through whom be glory to Him forever and ever. Amen. Therefore nothing is complete without Christ, because all things are through Him; when He is acknowledged, praise is given to God the Father through Him, because He is understood through Christ, as through His Wisdom, in whom He saved believers. Glory, therefore, to the Father through the Son, that is, glory to both in the Holy Spirit, because both are in one glory.

Romans 16:28 May the grace of our Lord Jesus Christ be with you all.

In conclusion, Paul places Christ, through whom we were made and by whose grace we were again reformed, so that he may remain fixed in our minds; for if we are mindful of his benefits, he will always protect us, as he said: “And behold,” he says (Matt. 28:20), “I am with you all days, even to the end of the age.” Amen.

APPENDIX

Commentary Romans 9:5

For when no mention is made of the Father’s name, and the discourse concerns Christ, it cannot be otherwise than that he is called God. Frequently, indeed, Scripture, because of its confession of one God, when it speaks of God the Father and joins the Son to God, calls the Father God and the Son Lord. But if anyone does not think that this was said of Christ: Who is God, let him identify the person of whom it was said: for no mention of God the Father has been made in this passage. But what wonder if in this passage he openly spoke of Christ as God over all, of whom elsewhere in the epistle Paul confirmed this meaning in such words, saying: That at the name of Jesus every knee should bow, of those in heaven, on earth, and under the earth (Phil. 2:10)? These are all things over which Christ is God. For there are no others, apart from these, so that Christ is not God over all; nor can every creature bow the knee except to God.

Questions (very close!)

But what wonder is it, when among other things he says to the Romans, “Theirs are the fathers, and from them, according to the flesh, is the Christ, who is God over all, blessed forever” (Rom. 9:5)? What does “from them, according to the flesh, is the Christ” mean? He would not have said this if He were not also according to God; so that, according to the flesh, He is from the fathers, but according to His divinity Christ is from God, God over all. But perhaps it might be said that this relates to the person of the Father? Yet in this passage there is no mention of the name of the Father. Therefore, if it is denied that this is said of Christ, let a person be given to whom it fittingly applies. And why should it not fit Christ, in whose name every knee bows in heaven and on earth and under the earth (Philip. 2:10)? that is, that He is God over all. For besides these there are no others. [1ST CATEGORY NT QUESTION 91.]

_____________

Commentary Romans 9:16

For when Saul sinned and asked forgiveness, he did not receive it (1 Kings 15:24); whereas David, having sinned and asked to be forgiven, obtained pardon (2 Sam. 12:8).

Questions (very close!)

Why is it that Saul, when he sinned, asked that prayer be made for him so that he might be forgiven and could not obtain it, while David, when he sinned, asked and obtained pardon? [1ST CATEGORY OT QUESTION 18.]

____________

Commentary Romans 4:3

It has shown that Abraham does not have glory before God because he was circumcised or because he kept himself from iniquity, but because he believed God; therefore he was justified and will obtain the reward of his praise in the future.

Questions

Nor was Abraham himself justified after he was circumcised: “Abraham,” it says, “believed God, and it was reckoned to him as righteousness” (Heb. 11:5, 7, 8; Rom. 4:3). Then he was circumcised, so that there might be in him a sign that, though he was old, he believed that he would have a son. Therefore it is not circumcision that commends Abraham, but faith. And so, for those who believe, faith is sufficient for justification, that they may be sons of Abraham in faith, not in circumcision, because they have no need of circumcision. [1ST CATEGORY OT QUESTION 44.]

_________________

Commentary Romans 4:13

For where there is no law, there is no transgression. Paul says this because, once the guilty are removed from the power of the law and pardon is given, there is no transgression; for those who through transgression of the law had been sinners are justified. For the law of deeds ceases, that is, the observance of Sabbaths, new moons, circumcision, distinction of foods, observance concerning a dead animal, or the blood of a weasel.

Questions

Now, because by a common name this Law which I called natural, and that which is called the Law of works, are both termed “law,” the Apostle appears to speak against the Law. But lest he seem to empty this Law, without which this life cannot be governed, he says, “We know that the Law is spiritual,” so that the Law which he annuls might be understood to be the Law of sabbath observance, circumcision, foods, and new moons. For that Law is therefore not called spiritual, because it punishes all sins. [2ND CATEGORY NT QUESTION 46.]

_______________

Commentary Romans 1:3

He, who was the Son of God according to the Holy Spirit, that is, according to God (for God is spirit, and is without doubt holy), says that he was made according to the flesh the Son of God from the seed of David, according to that: “And the Word was made flesh” (Jn. 1:14); so that Christ Jesus may now be one Son of God and of man, so that just as he is true God, he might also be true man; but he will not be true unless he is of flesh and soul, so that he may be perfect. For he, who from eternity was the Son of God, was unknown to the creature; but when he willed to be revealed for human salvation, he had to become visible and corporeal; because he also willed to be known by his power, and to cleanse man from sins in the flesh, with death overcome. And therefore he is made from the seed of David, so that just as he was born as king from God before the ages, so also according to the flesh he might receive royal origin from a king, made by the work of the Holy Spirit from the Virgin, that is, born; so that, with reverence reserved for him, he might from this be known as more than man; because he differed from the nativity under human law, just as it had been foretold by the prophet Isaiah: “Behold, a virgin shall conceive in the womb” (Isa. 7:14), so that when what was new should appear and worthy of praise, a certain providence of God concerning the visitation of the human race to come might be recognized.

Questions

AS WE CONFESS THAT THE SAVIOR WAS BORN, WHAT DOES IT MEAN THAT THE APOSTLE SAYS HE WAS MADE FROM THE SEED OF DAVID (Rom. 1:3), SINCE TO BE MADE IS ONE THING AND TO BE BORN ANOTHER? — Although in this place “making” can be understood as “birth”: for “making” does indeed differ from “generation,” but only in other contexts, not where the matter concerns flesh and body. Yet the Apostle did not use such words without reason; for in another place he says, “made of a woman” (Gal. 4:4). Therefore he meant something by this expression. For the flesh of the Lord in the Virgin’s womb was not formed and the body brought into being by human seed, but by the operation and power of the Holy Spirit; that is why the Apostle spoke in this way. It is one thing to thicken blood with mingled seed and therefore to generate, and another to bring forth, not by mixture, but by power. Therefore he said “made” rather than “begotten.” [2ND CATEGORY NT QUESTION 44.]

__________________

Commentary Romans 8:7

Paul did not say the flesh is hostile, but the wisdom of the flesh; that is, not the substance, but either evil actions, or thought, or assertion that is born of error. Therefore, the wisdom of the flesh is, in the first place, the speculation about the stars discovered by men, and then the delight in visible things. These are hostile to God, because they make the Lord of the elements and the maker of the world equal to the things He has made, asserting that nothing can be done beyond what the order of the world contains. Therefore they deny that God made it so that a Virgin gave birth, or that the bodies of the dead should rise again; because, they say, it is foolish that God should do anything beyond what man understands. O you wise of the world, who think that God ought not to act otherwise than does the creature made by Him, so that He Himself may be thought similar to the creatures! Therefore they are blinded, so that they do not see what an insult they offer to God. For the work which He agreed to do for the proclamation of His own praise, these men, disapproving it, declare to be unbelievable and foolish: and therefore this wisdom of the flesh cannot be subject to the law of God; for they sharpen their zeal to this end, that they may oppose the deeds of God.

Questions

For by saying “flesh” it has been made clear what is signified; from this it can easily be recognized what its wisdom is. We have said, then, that all the elements are called flesh, that is, the substances of visible things: whose way of thinking is such that they believe nothing can come about without a spiritual and simple mingling, while they shrink from the notion of power and judge it foolish. For those who, distrusting spiritual things, follow carnal ones, understand and consider as true nothing other than what is contained in the order of the elements: so that they neither admit that a virgin gave birth, nor believe in the resurrection of the flesh, because the reasoning of the flesh, that is, of the elements, does not accept this. For all things that are generated in time are produced by an operative mingling: and they say that dead and dissolved bodies cannot be restored again to life, because each element, by a separated mingling, returns to its own nature. Swollen with such notions, they deny that what we believe will happen or has happened: and therefore their assertion is hostile to God, since it considers as foolish and empty that which God has done and promised He will do. [2ND CATEGORY NT QUESTION 47.]

________________

Commentary Romans 11:29

For the gifts and calling of God are binding. It is true, because God’s grace in baptism does not seek groaning, or weeping, or any work, but only a profession from the heart. Nor, because they had grievously sinned by not accepting God’s promise, and because grave sins get forgiveness only through weeping and groaning, did he show that they could no longer receive mercy because he did not see them grieving; he shows that this is not required at the beginning of faith, for God’s gift freely forgives sins in baptism.

Questions

Accordingly, to those who turn to Him in faith He grants all sins together, without any preceding cry of penance. Therefore the Apostle Paul says: “For the gifts and the calling of God are without repentance” (Rom. 11:29). Afterwards, however, if they sin, they can deserve pardon through penance; so that, because they have sinned after receiving free mercy, they may no longer obtain it freely, but may reach forgiveness with intervening groans and tears. For it is impossible for a man not to sin. Therefore the Apostle John says: “If we say that we have no sin, we deceive ourselves, and the truth is not in us. If we confess our sins, He is faithful and just to forgive us our sins and to cleanse us from all unrighteousness” (1 Jn. 1:8). [1ST CATEGORY NT QUESTION 102. AGAINST NOVATIAN.]

________________

Commentary Romans 8:27

But he who searches the hearts knows what the Spirit desires, because he intercedes for the saints according to God’s will. It is clear that to God, to whom nothing is silent or hidden, the prayer of all spirits is known; how much more that of the Holy Spirit, who is certainly of the same substance, and speaks not by the impulse of air, nor like the angels, nor like the other creatures, but as befits his divinity! Therefore he speaks to God when he seems silent to us; for he also sees when he is not seen, and asks for those things which he knows please God and benefit us. Indeed, that same Spirit intercedes for us when he knows that through ignorance, not presumption, we ask for things contrary to our good.

Questions

For the deep mysteries of God are God’s inner realities, which cannot be searched by a creature. For it is also written that he intercedes for the saints according to God’s will, which the saints of course do not know, as the Apostle says: For we do not know what we should pray for as we ought (Rom. 8:27, 26). Therefore to know the deep things of God and not to be ignorant of the will of God signify one and the same thing. For what mystery is so deep as the will of God, which is known to the Holy Spirit, but is unknown to all others? We must also consider how it is said that no one knows what is in a man, except the spirit of the man which is in him (1 Cor. 2:11): by “spirit” he meant the soul; because no one knows what is in the soul of a man, except his soul, which is spirit. So also in God no one knows the things that are God’s, except the Spirit of God, that is, God himself. For one who is “of God” is understood to be nothing other than God. Therefore the Spirit of God knows what is in God, because he is of God. For if no one knows the thoughts of men except God, how much more can no one know God’s secrets and will unless he be of God, that is, the very same God? For he is known only to himself.

____________

Commentary Romans 5:12

Now death is the termination of the body, when the soul is separated from the body. There is also another death, which is called the second, in Gehenna, which we do not suffer by Adam’s sin, but, on the occasion of it, is acquired by our own sins: from this the good are free, only that they were in hell [inferno], but in the upper part, as if in a place of liberty, since they could not ascend to heaven;

Questions

For cleansed from sins by faith, and alienated from the second death (which, by the judgment pronounced on Adam, kept men in the hell [inferos]), held by none, he goes into the paradise of God the Father. [1ST CATEGORY OT QUESTION 47.]
